\tilde{h}_{3(\log 2)^{\frac{1}{3}}}(\phi,\theta)
and
\kappa(\phi,\theta)
.
Define

v_{D}(\rho e^{i\phi},\rho e^{i\theta})=\gamma(\rho).

•

Let

Z_{1}:=\{(z^{1},z^{2})\in\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}:|z^{1}|\geq|z^{2}|\}

and

\varphi_{1}:\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{w^{1}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{w^{2}}\rightarrow Z_{1}\subset\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}

be a homeomorphism defined by (see Figure
2
)

(z^{1},z^{2})=\varphi_{1}(w^{1},w^{2}),\ \ \ z^{1}=w^{1},\ z^{2}=|w^{1}|w^{2}.

Figure 2.
The map
\varphi_{1}
.

•

Define

v_{1}:\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{w^{1}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{w^{2}}\rightarrow(\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{w^{1}}^{*}}\times\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{w^{2}}})\times_{\rho^{\prime}}\tilde{X}

by setting
v_{1}
to be
the fiber-wise harmonic section with boundary values given by
v_{D}\circ\varphi_{1}\big|_{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{w^{1}}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\theta}}
. More precisely, we apply Theorem
7.1
as follows: For each
w^{1}\in\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{w^{1}}
, the restriction

v_{1,w^{1}}:=v_{1}\big|_{\{w^{1}\}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{w_{2}}}

is the unique harmonic section with boundary values

v_{1,w^{1}}\big|_{\mathbb{S}^{1}\approx\{w^{1}\}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\theta}}=v_{D}\circ\varphi_{1}\big|_{\mathbb{S}^{1}\approx\{w^{1}\}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\theta}}

as in Theorem
7.1
.

•

Similarly, let

Z_{2}:=\{(z^{1},z^{2})\in\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}:|z^{1}|\leq|z^{2}|\}

and

\varphi_{2}:\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{w^{1}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{w^{2}}\rightarrow Z_{2}\subset\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}

be the homeomorphism defined by

(z^{1},z^{2})=\varphi_{2}(w^{1},w^{2}),\ \ \ z^{1}=|w^{2}|w^{1},\ z^{2}=w^{2}.

We define

{v_{2}}:\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{w^{1}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{w^{2}}\rightarrow(\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{w^{1}}^{*}}\times\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{w^{2}}})\times_{\rho^{\prime}}\tilde{X}

by setting
v_{2}
to be
the fiber-wise harmonic section with boundary values given by
v_{D}\circ\varphi_{2}\big|_{\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\phi}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{w^{2}}}
.

•

Let

v:\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{1}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}\rightarrow(\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}^{*}}\times\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}})\times_{\rho^{\prime}}\tilde{X}

be the section defined by

\displaystyle v=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}v_{1}\circ\varphi_{1}^{-1}&\mbox{ on}\ \ Z_{1},\\
{v_{2}}\circ\varphi_{2}^{-1}&\mbox{ on}\ \ Z_{2}.\end{array}\right.

Note that
v
is well defined since
Z_{1}\cap Z_{2}=D
and

{v_{1}}\circ\varphi_{1}^{-1}\big|_{D}=v_{D}={v_{2}}\circ\varphi_{2}^{-1}\big|_{D}.

12.2.2.
Derivative estimates in a set of type (B)

Since
z^{1}=w^{1}
and
z^{2}=|w^{1}|w^{2}
, we have

(12.5)

w^{1}=z^{1}=\rho e^{i\phi},\ \ z^{2}=\rho e^{i\theta},\ \ w^{2}=\frac{z^{2}}{|z^{2}|}=\frac{r}{\rho}e^{i\theta}.

By (
12.3
),

\left|\frac{\partial h_{3(-\log\rho)^{\frac{1}{3}}}}{\partial\rho}(\theta,\phi)\right|^{2}=\left|\frac{\partial h_{t}}{\partial t}(\theta,\phi)\right|^{2}\left|\frac{\partial t}{\partial\rho}(\theta,\phi)\right|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\rho^{2}(-\log\rho)^{\frac{4}{3}}}.

Thus, noting that
w^{1}=z^{1}=\rho e^{i\phi}
,

\left|\frac{\partial(v_{D}\circ\varphi_{1})}{\partial\rho}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\rho^{2}(-\log\rho)^{\frac{4}{3}}}\mbox{ on $\mathbb{D}^{*}_{w^{1},\frac{1}{2}}\times\{|w^{2}|=1\}$}.

Furthermore, (
12.3
) implies

\left|\frac{\partial(v_{D}\circ\varphi_{1})}{\partial\phi}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{\mathsf{E}_{i}}{2\pi}(1+be^{-a(-\log\rho)})^{2}=\frac{\mathsf{E}_{i}}{2\pi}(1+b\rho^{a})^{2}\ \mbox{ on $\mathbb{D}^{*}_{w^{1},\frac{1}{2}}\times\{|w^{2}|=1\}$.}

Since
v_{1}
is a fiber-wise harmonic section, an argument analogous to the proof of first inequality of Lemma
12.1
(i.e. apply the maximum principle for subharmonic functions
d(u(\rho_{1}e^{i\phi}),u(\rho_{2}e^{i\phi}))
and
d(u(\rho e^{i\phi_{1}}),u(\rho e^{i\phi_{2}}))
) implies

\left|\frac{\partial{v_{1}}}{\partial\rho}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\rho^{2}(-\log\rho)^{\frac{4}{3}}}\ \mbox{ and }\left|\frac{\partial{v_{1}}}{\partial\phi}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{\mathsf{E}_{i}}{2\pi}(1+b\rho^{a})^{2}\ \ \mbox{ in }\mathbb{D}^{*}_{w^{1},\frac{1}{2}}\times\mathbb{D}^{*}_{w^{2}}.

Thus,

(12.6)

\left|\frac{\partial v_{1}}{\partial w^{1}}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\rho^{2}(-\log\rho)^{\frac{4}{3}}}+\frac{\mathsf{E}_{i}}{2\pi\rho^{2}}(1+b\rho^{a})^{2}\ \ \mbox{ in }\mathbb{D}^{*}_{w^{1},\frac{1}{2}}\times\mathbb{D}^{*}_{w^{1}}.

Since
\kappa
is a Lipschitz section,
v_{D}\circ\varphi_{1}
is a Lipschitz section for
\rho\geq\frac{1}{2}
. Thus,

(12.7)

\left|\frac{\partial v_{1}}{\partial w^{1}}\right|^{2}\leq C\ \ \mbox{ in }\mathbb{D}^{*}_{w^{1},\frac{1}{2},1}\times\mathbb{D}^{*}_{w^{2}}.

Furthermore, using the harmonicity of
v_{1}
restricted to the slice
\{w^{1}_{0}\}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{w^{2}}
, we have by Theorem
7.1
that

(12.8)

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{j}\log\frac{r_{0}}{r}\leq\int_{\{w^{1}_{0}\}\times\mathbb{D}_{r,r_{0}}}\left|\frac{\partial{v_{1}}}{\partial w^{2}}\right|^{2}\frac{dw^{2}\wedge d\bar{w}^{2}}{-2i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+\mathsf{E}_{j}\log\frac{r_{0}}{r}

for
0<r<r_{0}\leq\frac{1}{4}
.

Lemma 12.2

(Derivative estimates a set of type (B) away from the juncture)
.

For
\Omega:=\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4},1}
, there exists a constant
C
such that the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{1}}(z^{1}_{0},z^{2}_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C,\ \ \forall(z^{1}_{0},z^{2}_{0})\in\Omega\times\mathbb{D}^{*}

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{j}\log\frac{r_{0}}{r}\leq\int_{\{z^{1}_{0}\}\times\mathbb{D}_{r,r_{0}}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+\mathsf{E}_{j}\log\frac{r_{0}}{r}

where
0<r<r_{0}\leq\frac{1}{4}
,
z_{0}^{1}\in\Omega
and
z^{2}=re^{i\theta}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4},1}\times\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4}}^{*}\subset Z_{1}
, the estimates follow by applying the change of variables (
12.5
) to estimates (
12.6
), (
12.7
), (
12.8
) and noting that
\rho>\frac{1}{4}
in
\Omega
.
∎

Lemma 12.3

(Derivative estimates in a set of type (B) near the juncture)
.

For
v
restricted to
\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}
, there exists a constant
C
such that the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{j}\log\frac{r_{0}}{r}\leq\int_{\{z^{1}_{0}\}\times\mathbb{D}_{r,r_{0}}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+\mathsf{E}_{j}\log\frac{r_{0}}{r},

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{i}\log\frac{r_{0}}{r}\leq\int_{\mathbb{D}_{r,r_{0}}\times\{z^{2}_{0}\}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{-2i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+\mathsf{E}_{i}\log\frac{r_{0}}{r}

for
z^{1}_{0},z^{2}_{0}\in\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}
and
0<r<r_{0}\leq\frac{1}{4}
.

Proof.

We only prove the first estimate, the second being similar. First, note that the lower bound follows from the definition of
\mathsf{E}_{j}
. Next, we estimate the upper bound. For
z^{1}_{0}\in\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}
, we have the inclusion
\{z^{1}_{0}\}\times\mathbb{D}_{r,r_{0}}\subset Z_{1}
whenever
r_{0}\leq|z^{1}_{0}|
. Thus, the change of variables
w^{2}\mapsto z^{2}=|w^{1}|w^{2}
in (
12.8
) yields

\int_{\{z^{1}_{0}\}\times\mathbb{D}_{r,r_{0}}}\left|\frac{\partial{v}}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2i}\leq C+\mathsf{E}_{j}\log\frac{r_{0}}{r}

for
z^{1}_{0}\in\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}
and
0<r<r_{0}\leq|z_{0}^{1}|
.

If
r<|z_{0}^{1}|<r_{0}
,
we break up the integral into two integrals since the estimate for
\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}
is different in
Z_{2}
than in
Z_{1}
. Indeed, by an analogous argument to the proof of (
12.6
),
we have

\left|\frac{\partial v_{2}}{\partial w^{2}}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{r^{2}(-\log r)^{\frac{4}{3}}}+\frac{\mathsf{E}_{j}}{2\pi r^{2}}(1+r^{a})^{2}

After a change of variables
z^{1}=|w^{2}|w^{1},\ z^{2}=w^{2}
,

(12.9)

\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{r^{2}(-\log r)^{\frac{4}{3}}}+\frac{\mathsf{E}_{j}}{2\pi r^{2}}(1+r^{a})^{2}\ \ \mbox{ in }Z_{2}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\{z^{1}_{0}\}\times\mathbb{D}_{r,r_{0}}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\int_{r}^{|z_{0}^{1}|}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}\,rdr+\int_{|z_{0}^{1}|}^{r_{0}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}\,rdr\right)d\theta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(C+\mathsf{E}_{j}\log\frac{|z_{0}^{1}|}{r}\right)+\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|z_{0}^{1}|}^{r_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}(-\log r)^{\frac{4}{3}}}+\frac{\mathsf{E}_{j}}{2\pi r^{2}}(1+r^{a})^{2}\right)\ rdrd\theta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+\mathsf{E}_{j}\log\frac{r_{0}}{r}.

Finally, if
|z_{0}^{1}|<r<r_{0}
, we only use the estimate (
12.9
). We omit the details.
∎

12.3.
Gluing the maps

Given the homomorphism
\rho:\pi_{1}(M)\rightarrow\mathsf{Isom}(\tilde{X})
of Theorem
1
, we will construct a prototype section
v:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
.

Let
P\in\Sigma_{j}\cap\Sigma_{i}
and
U=\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{2}}
be a set of of type (B) with

\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}\simeq\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}\times\{0\}\subset\Sigma_{j}\ \mbox{ and }\ \bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{2}}\simeq\{0\}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{2}}\subset\Sigma_{i}

and

P=\{0\}\times\{0\}.

The identification of the product space

\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{2}}

as a subset of
M
is simultaneously induced by the local trivializations
of the disk bundles
\pi_{j}:\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{j}\rightarrow\Sigma_{j}
and
\pi_{i}:\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{i}\rightarrow\Sigma_{i}

via

\pi_{j}^{-1}(\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}})\simeq\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{2}}\ \mbox{ and }\ \pi_{i}^{-1}(\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{2}})\simeq\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{2}}

(cf. (
10.11
)).

Recall the following items associated with the set
U:=\mathbb{D}_{z^{1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{z^{2}}
.

•

[\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\theta^{k}}]
is the element of
\pi_{1}(\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{k}})
generated by
\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\theta^{k}}
for
k=1,2

•

[\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\theta^{1}}]
and
[\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\theta^{2}}]
also are the elements of
\pi_{1}(\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{1}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}})
generated by
\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\theta^{1}}\simeq\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\theta^{1}}\times\{z^{2}\}
and
\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\theta^{1}}\simeq\{z^{1}\}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\theta^{2}}
respectively

•

\pi_{1}(\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{k}}^{*})
is identified as a subgroup of
\pi_{1}(\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{1}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}})
for
k=1,2

•

\rho^{\prime}:\pi_{1}(\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{1}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{1}})\rightarrow\mathsf{Isom}(\tilde{X})
is a homomorphism and
\rho^{\prime}_{k}=\rho^{\prime}|_{\pi_{1}(\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{k}}^{*})}
for
k=1,2

•

(\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}^{*}}\times\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}})\times_{\rho^{\prime}}\tilde{X}\rightarrow\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}
and
\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{k}}^{*}}\times_{\rho^{\prime}_{k}}\tilde{X}\rightarrow\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{k}}^{*}
for
k=1,2
are fiber bundles.

Next, let

V=\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z_{2}}
be a set of type (A) with

\Omega\simeq\Omega\times\{0\}\subset\Sigma_{j}.

The identification
V\simeq\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}
is induced by the local trivialization

\pi_{j}^{-1}(\Omega)\simeq\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{2}}

of the bundle
\pi_{j}:\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{j}\rightarrow\Sigma_{j}
(cf. (
10.9
)).
If
U\cap V\neq\emptyset
(and hence

\Omega\cap\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}\neq\emptyset
),
then the transition function of the disk bundle
\pi_{j}:\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{j}\rightarrow\Sigma_{j}
defines a smooth map

\tau:\Omega\cap\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}\rightarrow U(1).

By
[
KS1
, Proposition 2.6.1]
, there exists a locally Lipschitz section
k:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
of the fiber bundle
\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}\rightarrow X
.
Let
k_{U}
be the lift to
(\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}^{*}}\times\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}})\times_{\rho^{\prime}}\tilde{X}
of the restriction
of
k
to
U^{*}:=\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{1}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}
and let

v_{U}:U^{*}:=\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{1}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}\rightarrow(\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{1}}}\times\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}})\times_{\rho^{\prime}}\tilde{X}

be the local prototype section defined in Subsection
12.2
.
The composition of
v_{U}
and the quotient map

\tilde{M}\times_{\rho^{\prime}}\tilde{X}\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
defines
a section
of
\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}\rightarrow U
which we call again
v_{U}
.

Also
let
k_{V}
be the lift to
(\Omega\times\widetilde{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}})\times_{\rho^{\prime}_{2}}\tilde{X}
of the restriction of
k
to
V^{*}=\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}
and let

v_{V}:V^{*}:=\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{z^{2}}\rightarrow(\Omega\times\widetilde{\mathbb{D}^{*}_{z^{2}}})\times_{\rho^{\prime}_{2}}\tilde{X}

be the local prototype section defined in Subsection
12.1.1
. The composition of
v_{V}
and the quotient map

\tilde{M}\times_{\rho^{\prime}_{2}}\tilde{X}\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
defines
a section
of
\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}\rightarrow V
which we call again
v_{V}
.

We claim that we can glue these local sections together to define
v
in
U\cup V
. To do so, we have to show the following.

Lemma 12.4
.

If
U
and
V
are sets of type (B) and (A) respectively, then the sections
v_{U}
and
v_{V}
agree on
U^{*}\cap V^{*}
.

Proof.

For
p\in\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{1}}\cap\Omega
, let
v_{U,p}
,
k_{U,p}
be the restrictions of
v_{U}
,
k_{U}
respectively to

\{p\}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{2}}^{*}
and
v_{V,p}
,
k_{V,p}
be the restrictions of
v_{V}
,
k_{V}
respectively to
\{p\}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{z^{2}}^{*}
. We claim that the harmonic sections
v_{U,p}
and
v_{V,p}
are related by the transition relation

v_{U,p}=v_{V,p}\circ\tau(p).

Indeed, since multiplication by
\tau(p)\in U(1)
is a conformal map,

v_{V,p}\circ\tau(p)
is harmonic on
\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}
with boundary values
k_{U,p}=k_{V,p}\circ\tau(p)\big|_{\partial\mathbb{D}\simeq\{p\}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}_{\theta}}
. The assertion follows from the uniqueness of harmonic maps (cf. Theorem
7.1
).
∎

Similar construction holds for two sets of type (A).

Lemma 12.5
.

If
U
and
V
are both of type (A) and
U\cap V\neq\emptyset
, then
v_{U}
and
v_{V}
agree on
U^{*}\cap V^{*}
.

Proof.

Apply the same argument as Lemma
12.4
.
∎

Let
{\mathcal{U}}
be a finite open cover of
\bar{\mathcal{D}}
by sets of type (A) and of type (B). Let
{\mathcal{U}}_{A}\subset{\mathcal{U}}
be the collection of sets of type (A) and
{\mathcal{U}}_{B}\subset{\mathcal{U}}
be the collections of sets of type (B). Without the loss of generality, we can assume
{\mathcal{U}}_{B}
is a collection of disjoint sets. Set

(12.10)

v=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}v_{U}&\mbox{in }U\cap{\mathcal{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}\mbox{ where }U\in{\mathcal{U}}_{B}\\
v_{V}&\mbox{in }(V\cap{\mathcal{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}})\backslash\bigcup_{U\in{\mathcal{U}}_{B}}U\mbox{ where }V\in{\mathcal{U}}_{A}\end{array}\right.

and extend to the rest of
M
as a well-defined, locally Lipschitz global section of
\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}\rightarrow M
.

Definition 12.6
.

The map

(12.11)

v:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times\tilde{X}

constructed above is called the
prototype section
.
The corresponding
\rho
-equivariant map
\tilde{v}:\tilde{M}\rightarrow\tilde{X}
is called the
prototype map
.

13.
Energy estimates of the prototype section

The goal of this section is to obtain the energy estimates of the prototype section

v:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}

of Definition
12.6
with respect to the Poincaré-type metric
g
given by Definition
11.1
.
Throughout this section, we use
C
to denote constants that are independent of the distance to the divisor. (Note that
C
may change from line to line.)

We consider the following three types of sets intersecting the divisor
\Sigma\subset\bar{M}
:

(i)

\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}\subset\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
, a subset in a set of type (A)

(ii)

\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}:=\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4},1}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}\subset\bar{\mathbb{D}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
, a subset in a set of type (B) away from the crossing (cf. Figure
3
)

(iii)

\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}\subset\bar{\mathbb{D}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
, a subset in a set of type (B) at the crossing (cf. Figure
3
)

Figure 3.
Subsets of a set of type (B).

A neighborhood of
\Sigma
can be covered by a
finite collection of sets
of the above type. In order to estimate the energy of
v
, we will compute its energy in each such set.

13.1.
Energy in a set of type (A)

In this subsection we will use the following notation in addition to the one used in Subsection
12.1
.

•

\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}
is the subset of
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
with
0<r_{1}<|z^{2}|<r_{2}<\frac{1}{4}
.

•

g_{\Sigma_{j}}
is the smooth metric on
\Omega
as defined in Definition
11.2

•

\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}
is the area with respect to
g_{\Sigma_{j}}

•

P
is the product metric on
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}
defined by (
11.15
).

Note that

{\mathcal{D}}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\cap(\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}})=\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\ \ \mbox{(cf.~(\ref{nbhd12}))}.

The strategy for estimating the energy of the prototype section
v
in
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
will be to first compute the energy of
v
with respect to the product metric
P
(cf. (
11.15
)). Since the metrics
P
and
g
are close (cf. (
11.16
)), this will give us the estimate of the energy of
v
with respect to
g
.

Lemma 13.1
.

For a subset
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
of a set of type (A), there exists a constant
C>0
such that the energy with respect to the metric
P
of the prototype section
v
satisfies

\displaystyle 0\leq{{}^{P}E}^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]-\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\Omega)\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\leq C,\ \ \ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Proof.

By (
11.2
),

{}^{P}E^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}}P^{1\bar{1}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}+P^{2\bar{2}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{P}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}}P^{1\bar{1}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{P}+\int_{\Omega}\left(\int_{\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2i}\right)d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}},

hence the inequality on the right follows from Lemma
12.1
(cf Remark
11.5
).

By the definition of
\mathsf{E}_{j}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\Omega)\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{E}_{j}\frac{dr}{r}d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\int_{\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}\frac{dr\wedge d\theta}{r}\right)d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}}P^{1\bar{1}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}+P^{2\bar{2}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{P}

\displaystyle=

{}^{P}E^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}],

which proves the inequality on the left.
∎

Lemma 13.2
.

For a subset
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
of a set type (A), there exists a constant
C>0
such that the prototype section
v
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|{}^{g}E^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]-\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\Omega)\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right|\leq C,\ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Proof.

By Lemma
13.1
, it suffices to show that the difference of the energy of
v
with respect to
g
and with respect to
P
is bounded; i.e.

\left|{}^{g}E^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]-{{}^{P}E}^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]\right|\leq C.

We obtain the above estimate with the help of Lemma
16.1
found in the Appendix to this chapter. Therefore we need to first show that the assumption (
16.1
) of Lemma
16.1
is satisfied; in other words, we need an estimate of the intergral of
\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}
. Below, we will derive the estimate (
16.1
) by bounding the
z^{1}
-energy and
r
-energy of
v
and then subtracting those from the full energy estimate of Lemma
13.1
.

First, to bound the
z^{1}
-energy of
v
, we use estimate
\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}\leq C
of (
12.1
) to see that

\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2ir^{2}(\log r^{2}+A)^{2}}\leq C.

Second, to bound the
r
-energy of
v
,
use estimate
\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial r}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{C}{r^{2}(-\log r)}
from Theorem
7.1
to see that

\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial r}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2i}\leq C.

Thus, Lemma
13.1
and the identities for
P^{1\bar{1}}d\mbox{vol}_{P}
and
P^{2\bar{2}}d\mbox{vol}_{P}
given by (
11.2
) imply the following integral estimate on
\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}
:

\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2i}-\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\Omega)\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\leq C.

We set
r_{2}=\frac{1}{4}
and let
r_{1}\rightarrow 0
above to obtain

\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}-\mathsf{E}_{j}\right)d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dr\wedge d\theta}{r}\leq C.

Noting that
g_{\Sigma_{j}}
is a smooth metric, the assumption (
16.1
) of Lemma
16.1
is satsified. Consequently (noting that
A
appears in the metric expression of
P
is a bounded function),

(13.1)

\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dr\wedge d\theta}{r(\log r^{2}+A)^{2}}\leq C^{\prime}

where the constant
C^{\prime}
depends only on
C
.

We use the above estimate to compute the difference between
{}^{g}E^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]
and
{}^{P}E^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]
. The trickiest to bound include the following two terms for which we use the estimate (
13.1
):

\bullet

\displaystyle{\left|\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4}}}P^{\theta\theta}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}(d\mbox{vol}_{g}-d\mbox{vol}_{P})\right|}

To bound this term, note that since by (
11.17
)

d\mbox{vol}_{P}-d\mbox{vol}_{g}=O\left(\frac{1}{(-\log r^{2}+A)^{2}}\right)d\mbox{vol}_{P}=O(1)d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2ir^{2}(-\log r^{2}+A)^{4}}

and

P^{2\bar{2}}=r^{2}(\log r^{2}+A)^{2}.

and thus

P^{2\bar{2}}(d\mbox{vol}_{P}-d\mbox{vol}_{g})=O(1)d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2i(-\log r^{2}+A)^{2}}.

Thus,

P^{\theta\theta}\left(d\mbox{vol}_{P}-d\mbox{vol}_{g}\right)=d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2ir^{2}(-\log r^{2}+A)^{2}}=d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dr\wedge d\theta}{r(-\log r^{2}+A)^{2}}

which in turn implies

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4}}}P^{\theta\theta}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}(d\mbox{vol}_{g}-d\mbox{vol}_{P})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dr\wedge d\theta}{r(-\log r^{2}+A)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CC^{\prime}.

\bullet

\displaystyle{\left|\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(g^{\theta\theta}-P^{\theta\theta}\right)\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{P}\right|}

To bound this term, note that since
g^{2\bar{2}}-P^{2\bar{2}}=O(r^{2})
, we have

g^{\theta\theta}-P^{\theta\theta}=O(1)\mbox{ and }d\mbox{vol}_{P}=d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2ir^{2}(\log r^{2}+A)^{2}}

by (
11.2
). Combining the above, we obtain

\left(g^{\theta\theta}-P^{\theta\theta}\right)d\mbox{vol}_{P}=O(1)d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dr\wedge d\theta}{r(-\log r^{2}+A)^{2}}.

Thus

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(g^{\theta\theta}-P^{\theta\theta}\right)\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{P}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dr\wedge d\theta}{r(-\log r^{2}+A)^{2}}\leq CC^{\prime}.

The other terms of
\left|{}^{g}E^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]-{{}^{P}E}^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]\right|
are also bounded by similar computations. We omit the details.
∎

Lemma 13.3
.

For a subset
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
of a set type (A), there exists a constant
C>0
such that the prototype section
v
satisfies

{}^{g}E^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]\leq{{}^{g}E^{f}}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]+C,\ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}

for any locally Lipschitz section
f:\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
.

Proof.

Since
\gamma_{j}
is minimizing,

(13.2)

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\Omega)\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left|\frac{\partial f}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}d\theta\int_{\Omega}d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\frac{dr}{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}}\left|\frac{\partial f}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dr\wedge d\theta}{r}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}}P^{1\bar{1}}\left|\frac{\partial f}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}+P^{2\bar{2}}\left|\frac{\partial f}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}\ d\mbox{vol}_{P}

\displaystyle=

{}^{P}E^{f}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}].

Thus, the desired estimate with
g
replaced by
P
follows from combining the above estimate with Lemma
13.1
.
Thus, we are left to show that

\left|{{}^{g}E^{f}}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]-{{}^{P}E^{f}}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]\right|\leq C.

To do so, note that if the inequality

\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial f}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{j}}{2\pi}\right)d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dr\wedge d\theta}{r}<\infty

does not hold, then we are done since the desired estimate holds by Lemma
13.2
. Hence, we can assume the above inequality and apply Lemma
16.1
to conclude

\int_{\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial f}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}\wedge\frac{dr\wedge d\theta}{r(\log r^{2}+A)^{2}}\leq C^{\prime}

where the constant
C^{\prime}
depends only on
C
.
The rest of the proof is exactly as in the proof of Lemma
13.1
.
∎

13.2.
Energy in a set of Type (B) away from the crossing

In this subsection we will use the following notation in addition to the one used in Subsection
12.2
.

•

\Omega:=\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4},1}

•

\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}
is the subset of
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}=\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4},1}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
with
0<r_{1}<|z^{2}|<r_{2}<\frac{1}{4}
.

•

g_{\Sigma_{j}}
is the smooth metric on
\Omega
as in Definition
11.2

•

\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}
is the area with respect to
g_{\Sigma_{j}}

•

P
is the product metric on
\bar{\mathbb{D}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
defined by (
11.20
).

Since
\Omega:=\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4},1}\subset\mathbb{D}
, the points of
\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}
are uniformly away from the crossing. In particular, since

\frac{1}{4}<\rho\mbox{ in $\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}$},

the metric expressions of
g
in a set of type (A) and of type (B) (cf. (
11.13
) and (
11.3
) respectively) show that
g
restricted to
\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}
in set type (B) has the same asymptotic behavior as
r\rightarrow 0
as
g
in a set of type (A). Thus, the procedure for obtaining energy estimates of
v
will be analogous to that in the previous subsection.
Note that

{\mathcal{D}}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\cap(\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}))=\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\mbox{ for $0<r_{1}<r_{2}<\frac{1}{4}$ (cf.~(\ref{nbhd12}))}.

Lemma 13.4
.

For a subset
\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}
of a set of type (B) (with
\Omega=\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4},1}
), there exists a constant
C>0
such that the prototype section
v
satisfies

{}^{P}E^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]\leq\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\Omega)\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}+C,\ \ \ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Proof.

Follow the proof of Lemma
13.1
but by replacing (
11.2
) by (
11.3
) and Lemma
12.1
by Lemma
12.2
. (Note that
\frac{1}{4}<\rho
, i.e.
\rho
is bounded away from 0, so the expressions in (
11.2
) and (
11.3
) are comparable.)
∎

Lemma 13.5
.

For a subset
\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}
of a set of type (B) (with
\Omega=\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4},1}
), there exists a constant
C>0
such that the prototype section
v
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|{}^{g}E^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]-\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\Omega)\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right|\leq C,\ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Proof.

Follow the proof of Lemma
13.2
using Lemma
13.4
instead of Lemma
13.1
.
∎

Lemma 13.6
.

For a subset
\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}
of a set of type (B) (with
\Omega=\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4},1}
), there exists a constant
C>0
such that the prototype section
v
satisfies

{}^{g}E^{v}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]\leq{{}^{g}E^{f}}[\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]+C,\ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}

for any locally Lipschitz section
f:\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
.

Proof.

Follow the proof of Lemma
13.3
using Lemma
13.5
instead of Lemma
13.2

∎

13.3.
Energy in a set of Type
(B)
at the crossing

In this subsection we will use the following notation in addition the one used in Subsection
12.2
.

•

\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}
is the subset of
\bar{\mathbb{D}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
with
0<r_{1}<|z^{k}|<r_{2}<\frac{1}{4}
for
k=1,2
.

•

g_{\Sigma_{j}}
,
g_{\Sigma_{i}}
are as in Definition
11.2

•

\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}
and
\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{i}}}
are the areas with respect to
g_{\Sigma_{i}}

•

P
is the product metric on
\bar{\mathbb{D}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
defined by (
11.20
).

The goal is to estimate the energy of
v
in the set

U_{r_{1},r_{2}}=(\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}})\backslash(\mathbb{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}}),

pictured in Figure
4
.

Figure 4.
The region
U_{r_{1},r_{2}}
.

The procedure for doing so involves an extra step compared to the procedure in the previous two subsections. Namely, we will first derive an expression for the energy with respect to the product metric
P
in the box
\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}
pictured in Figure
5
(cf. Lemma
13.9
below).

Figure 5.
The region
\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}
.

Then we take the difference of the energy with respect to
P
contained in
\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}
and in
\mathbb{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}}
to bound the energy in
U_{r_{1},r_{2}}
(cf. Lemma
13.10
below).
Finally, since the difference between the metric
g
and
P
is small, we obtain a bound for the energy with respect to
g
in
U_{r_{1},r_{2}}
(cf. Lemma
13.11
below).

Definition 13.7
.

We define
P_{\Sigma_{i}}
,
P_{\Sigma_{j}}
to be the restriction of
P
(cf. (
11.20
)) to
\Sigma_{i}
,
\Sigma_{j}
respectively.

Remark 13.8
.

Note that
\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r,r^{\prime}})=\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r,r^{\prime}})
for
0<r<r^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{4}
by the symmetry of
P
.

Lemma 13.9
.

In a subset
\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}
of a set
\mathbb{D}^{*}\times\mathbb{D}^{*}
of type (B), the prototype section
v
satisfies

\left|{}^{P}E^{v}[\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}]-\left(\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}})\log{\frac{1}{4r}}+\mathsf{E}_{i}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}})\log{\frac{1}{4r}}\right)\right|\leq C

for
0<r<\frac{1}{4}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
12.3
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{i}\log\frac{r_{0}}{r}\leq\int_{\mathbb{D}_{r,r_{0}}\times\{z^{2}_{0}\}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{-2i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+\mathsf{E}_{i}\log\frac{r_{0}}{r}

for
z^{2}_{0}\in\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}
and
0<r<r_{0}
. By (
11.3
),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}}P^{1\bar{1}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{P}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{-2i}\right)\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2ir^{2}(\log r^{2})^{2}}.

Combining the above, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{i}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}})\log{\frac{1}{4r}}\leq\int_{\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}}P^{1\bar{1}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{P}\leq\mathsf{E}_{i}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}})\log{\frac{1}{4r}}+C

and similarly

\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}})\log{\frac{1}{4r}}\leq\int_{\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}}}P^{2\bar{2}}\left|\frac{\partial v}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}d\mbox{vol}_{P}\leq\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}})\log{\frac{1}{4r}}+C.

∎

Lemma 13.10
.

In a subset
\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}
of a set of type (B), there exists a constant
C>0
such that in any
subset (cf. (
10.6
))

U_{r_{1},r_{2}}={\mathcal{D}}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\cap(\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}),\ \ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4},

the prototype section
v
satisfies

\displaystyle 0\leq{{}^{P}E^{v}[}U_{r_{1},r_{2}}]-\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}-\mathsf{E}_{i}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\leq C.

Proof.

By a straightforward computation,

\displaystyle\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{1}}-\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{1}}-\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{2}}

\displaystyle+\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{2}}-\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}+\left(\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}})\right)\log\frac{1}{4r_{2}}.

The second term is bounded by

\displaystyle 0\leq\left(\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}})\right)\log\frac{1}{4r_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\frac{dr}{r(\log r^{2})^{2}}\right)\log\frac{1}{4r_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\log r_{2}}-\frac{1}{\log r_{1}}\right)\log 4r_{2}\leq 1.

Thus, combining the above equality and the inequality and then multiplying by
\mathsf{E}_{j}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{1}}-\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{j}\left(\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}+1\right).\hskip 108.405pt

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{i}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{1}}-\mathsf{E}_{i}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{i}\left(\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}+1\right).\hskip 108.405pt

Furthermore, Lemma
13.9
implies

{}^{P}E^{v}[U_{r_{1},r_{2}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{}^{P}E}^{v}[\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}]-{{}^{P}E}^{v}[\mathbb{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}}\times\mathbb{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{1}}+\mathsf{E}_{i}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{1}}

\displaystyle\ -\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{2}}-\mathsf{E}_{i}\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{1}{4r_{2}}+C.

Thus, the desired estimate follows from the the fact that
\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}})=\mbox{Area}_{P_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}_{r,\frac{1}{4}})
(cf. Remark
13.8
).
∎

Lemma 13.11
.

In a subset
\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}
of a set
\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}
of type (B), there exists a constant
C>0
such that in any
subset (cf. (
10.6
))

U_{r_{1},r_{2}}={\mathcal{D}}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\cap(\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}),\ \ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4},\

the prototype section
v
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|{}^{g}E^{v}[U_{r_{1},r_{2}}]-\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}-\mathsf{E}_{i}\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{i}}}(\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}})\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right|\leq C.

Proof.

Follows from the metric estimate (
11.31
)
and Lemma
13.10
.
∎

Lemma 13.12
.

In a subset
\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}
of a set
\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}
of type (B), there exists a constant
C>0
such that in any
subset (cf. (
10.6
))

U_{r_{1},r_{2}}={\mathcal{D}}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\cap(\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}),\ \ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4},

the prototype section
v
satisfies

{}^{g}E^{v}[U_{r_{1},r_{2}}]\leq{{}^{g}E^{f}}[U_{r_{1},r_{2}}]+C

for any locally Lipschitz section
f:U_{r_{1},r_{2}}\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
.

Proof.

Follow the proof of Lemma
13.3
using Lemma
13.11
instead of Lemma
13.2

∎

13.4.
Energy estimates for the prototype section near the divisor

Combining the results of the previous three subsections, we obtain the following estimate in an open set
{\mathcal{D}}_{r_{1},r_{2}}
(cf. (
10.6
)) near the divisor.

Proposition 13.13
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that the prototype section
v
of Definition
12.6
satisfies

\left|{{}^{g}E^{v}}[{\mathcal{D}}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]-\sum_{j=1}^{L}\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\Sigma_{j})\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right|<C,\ \ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Proof.

Since we can cover a neighborhood of
\Sigma
by a finite collection of sets of type (A) and type (B), the estimate follows from Lemma
13.2
, Lemma
13.5
and Lemma
13.11
.
∎

Proposition 13.14
.

The section
v
is almost minimizing in
M
in the following sense: There exists a constant
C>0
such that

{}^{g}E^{v}[{\mathcal{D}}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]\leq{{}^{g}E^{f}}[{\mathcal{D}}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]+C,\ \ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}

for any section
f:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
.

Proof.

Since we can cover a neighborhood of
\Sigma
by a finite collection of sets of type (A) and type (B), the estimate, the estimate follows from Lemma
13.3
, Lemma
13.6
and Lemma
13.12
.
∎

14.
Harmonic maps of possibly infinite energy

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
9.8
, the existence of a harmonic map of logarithmic energy growth.
In Subsection
14.1
, we show the existence of a harmonic map with the help of the prototype map. In Subsection
14.2
, we record the energy growth estimates for this map.

Throughout this section, we use
C
to denote constants that are independent of the parameter
r
. Note that
C
may change from line to line.

14.1.
Proof of existence, Theorem
9.8

Proof.

For
r\in(0,\frac{1}{4}]
, let
M_{r}=M\backslash{\mathcal{D}}_{r}
(see Figure
6
).

Figure 6.
The region
M_{r}\subset M
.

Next, let
v:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
be the prototype section of Definition
12.6
and
let

u_{r}:M_{r}\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}

be the energy minimizer among all sections that agree with
v
on

\partial M_{r}
for each
r\in(0,r_{1}]
.
The existence of such a section
u_{r}

follows from the proof of
[
KS1
]
Theorem 2.7.2.

Since

{}^{g}E^{u_{r}}[{\mathcal{D}}_{r,r_{1}}]+{{}^{g}E^{u_{r}}}[M_{r_{1}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{}^{g}E^{u_{r}}}[M_{r}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{{}^{g}E^{v}}[M_{r}]\ \ \ \mbox{ (since $u_{r}$ is minimizing in $M_{r}$)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{}^{g}E^{v}}[{\mathcal{D}}_{r,r_{1}}]+{{}^{g}E^{v}}[M_{r_{1}}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{{}^{g}E^{u_{r}}}[{\mathcal{D}}_{r,r_{1}}]+C+{{}^{g}E^{v}}[M_{r_{1}}]\ \ \ \ \mbox{(by Proposition~\ref{comparison})},

we have that

(14.1)

{{}^{g}E^{u_{r}}}[M_{r_{1}}]\leq{{}^{g}E^{v}}[M_{r_{1}}]+C.

The right hand side of the inequality (
14.1
) is independent of the parameter
r
; i.e. once we fix
r_{1}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}]
, the quantity
{}^{g}E^{u_{r}}[M_{r_{1}}]
is uniformly bounded for all
r\in(0,r_{1}]
. This implies a uniform Lipschitz bound, say
L
, of
u_{r}
for
r\in(0,r_{1}]
in
M_{2r_{1}}
(cf.
[
KS1
, Theorem 2.4.6]
).

Let
\tilde{u}_{r}
and
\tilde{v}
be the
\rho
-equivariant maps corresponding to sections
u_{r}
and
v
.
Thus,

d(\tilde{u}_{r}(\lambda(p)),\tilde{u}_{r}(p))\leq Ld_{\tilde{M}}(\lambda(p),p),\ \ p\in M_{2r_{1}},\ \lambda\in\Lambda,\ \ r\in(0,r_{1}].

If we let

c=L\max\{d_{\tilde{M}}(\lambda(p),p):\lambda\in\Lambda,\ p\in\overline{M_{2r_{1}}}\},

then by equivariance

d(\rho(\lambda)\tilde{u}_{r}(p),\tilde{u}_{r}(p)))\leq c,\ \ \ p\in M_{2r_{1}},\ \lambda\in\Lambda,\ \ r\in(0,r_{1}].

In other words,
\delta(\tilde{u}_{r}(p))\leq L
for all
p\in M_{2r_{1}}
and
r\in(0,r_{1}]
.
By the properness of
\rho
, there exists
P_{0}\in\tilde{X}
and
R_{0}>0
such that

\{\tilde{u}_{r}(p):\ p\in M_{2r_{1}},\ r\in(0,r_{1}]\}\subset B_{R_{0}}(P_{0}).

Thus, by taking a compact exhaustion, applying Arzela-Ascoli and the usual diagonalization argument, there exists a subsequence of
\tilde{u}_{r}
that converges locally uniformly to a
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map
\tilde{u}:\tilde{M}\rightarrow\tilde{X}
. Let
u:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
be the corresponding harmonic section.
∎

14.2.
Energy estimates for the harmonic section

Lemma 14.1
.

For the harmonic section
u:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
of Theorem
9.8
and the prototype section of Definition
12.6
, we have

{{}^{g}E^{u}}[M_{r_{1}}]\leq{{}^{g}E^{v}}[M_{r_{1}}]+C,\ \ \ \forall r_{1}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].

Proof.

Follows from (
14.1
) and the lower semicontinuity of energy (cf.
[
KS1
, Lemma 1.6.1]
).
∎

Lemma 14.2
.

If
v:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
is the prototype section of Definition
12.6
and
u:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
is the harmonic section of Theorem
9.8
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\left|{{}^{g}E^{u}}[\mathcal{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]-{{}^{g}E^{v}}[\mathcal{D}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]\right|\leq C,\ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Proof.

From the fact that
\mathcal{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}\subset\mathcal{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}\cup M_{\frac{1}{4}}=M_{r_{1}}
, Lemma
14.1
, and the lower semicontinuity of energy (cf.
[
KS1
, Theorem 1.6.1]
), we obtain

{{}^{g}E^{u}}[\mathcal{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}]\leq{{}^{g}E^{u}}[M_{r_{1}}]\leq{{}^{g}E^{v}}[M_{r_{1}}]+C={{}^{g}E^{v}}[\mathcal{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}]+{{}^{g}E^{v}}[M_{\frac{1}{4}}]+C.

Proposition
13.14
implies

{{}^{g}E^{v}}[\mathcal{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}]\leq{{}^{g}E^{u}}[\mathcal{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}]+C.

Combining the above two inequalities we obtain

\left|{{}^{g}E^{u}}[\mathcal{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}]-{{}^{g}E^{v}}[\mathcal{D}_{r_{1},\frac{1}{4}}]\right|\leq{{}^{g}E^{v}}[M_{\frac{1}{4}}]+C

and similarly

\left|{{}^{g}E^{u}}[\mathcal{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}}]-{{}^{g}E^{v}}[\mathcal{D}_{r_{2},\frac{1}{4}}]\right|\leq{{}^{g}E^{v}}[M_{\frac{1}{4}}]+C.

The desired estimate follows from the above two inequalities.
∎

Lemma 14.3
.

If
u:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
is the harmonic section of Theorem
9.8
, then there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|{}^{g}E^{u}[{\mathcal{D}}_{r_{1},r_{2}}]-\sum_{j=1}^{L}\mathsf{E}_{j}\mbox{Area}_{g_{\Sigma_{j}}}(\Sigma_{j})\log\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C,\ \ \ 0<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Proof.

The estimate follows from Proposition
13.13
and Lemma
14.2
.
∎

Lemma 14.4
.

If
u:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
is the harmonic section of Theorem
9.8
, then we have the following estimates in the subset
\Omega\times\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}
of a set
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
of type (A) or the subset
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}:=\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4},1}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}
of a set
\bar{\mathbb{D}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
of type (B):

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}(-\log r^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{j}}{8\pi r^{2}}\right)dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial r}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{j}}{2\pi}\right)dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}(-\log r^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{(-\log r^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

where
(z^{1},z^{2}=re^{i\theta})
are the standard product coordinates on
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
.

Proof.

All the estimates except for the last two follow immediately from Lemma
14.3
. The last two follow from the other estimates and Lemma
16.1
.∎

Recall that the standard product coordinates
(z^{1},z^{2})
on a set
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
of type (A) are not necessarily the holomorphic coordinates
(z^{1},\zeta)
of Definition
11.3
. We will now reframe the statements of Lemma
14.4
in terms of the holomorphic coordinates on the set of type (A). We first need some estimates that compares
\zeta
to
z^{2}
.

Lemma 14.5
.

If
(z^{1},z^{2}=re^{i\theta})
and
(z^{1},\zeta=se^{i\eta})
are the standard product coordinates and holomorphic coordinates respectively on a set
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
of type (A), then

\displaystyle r=as+O(r^{2})

\displaystyle\frac{\partial z^{2}}{\partial\zeta}=a(1+O(r))

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\bar{z}^{2}}{\partial\zeta}=O(r)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial r}{\partial s}=|\alpha|(1+O(r))

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta}{\partial s}=O(1)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial r}{\partial\eta}=O(r^{2})

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta}{\partial\eta}=O(1)

where
a
is a smooth function both bounded above and bounded away from 0 (cf. (
11.7
) and (
11.8
)).

Proof.

Since

z^{2}=a\zeta+O(r^{2})

by (
11.8
), the first estimate follows immediately.
Furthermore, differentiating the above with respect to
\zeta
, we obtain the next two estimates.
The last four estimates are obtained
by evaluating the differential forms of (
11.2
) on the vector fields

\frac{\partial}{\partial s}
,

\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}

and using the fact that

|\frac{\partial}{\partial s}|=O(1)
,

|\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}|=O(r^{2})
.
∎

Theorem 14.6
.

If
u:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
is the harmonic section of Theorem
9.8
, then we have the following estimates in the set
\Omega\times\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}
away from a crossing (i.e. a subset of a set
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
of type (A) or a subset
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}:=\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4},1}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}
of a set
\bar{\mathbb{D}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
of type (B)),

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{s^{2}(-\log s^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\zeta}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{j}}{8\pi s^{2}}\right)dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\zeta}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{(-\log s^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial s}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\eta}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{j}}{2\pi}\right)dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{s^{2}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\eta}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{s^{2}(-\log s^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

where
(z^{1},\zeta=se^{i\eta})
are the holomorphic coordinates on
\Omega\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
(cf. Definition
11.3
).

Proof.

By Lemma
14.5
, we have

\displaystyle s^{2}(\log s^{2})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{2}(\log r^{2})O(1),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{s^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{|a|}{r^{2}}(1+O(r)),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u}{\partial\zeta}\

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{2}}\frac{\partial z^{2}}{\partial\zeta}+\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{2}}\frac{\partial\bar{z}^{2}}{\partial\zeta}=\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{2}}a(1+O(r))+\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{2}}O(r),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u}{\partial\eta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u}{\partial r}\frac{\partial r}{\partial\eta}+\frac{\partial u}{\partial\theta}\frac{\partial\theta}{\partial\eta}=\frac{\partial u}{\partial r}O(r^{2})+\frac{\partial u}{\partial\theta}O(1).

Thus,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\zeta}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{j}}{8\pi s^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|a|^{2}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{j}}{8\pi r^{2}}\right)(1+O(r))

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\eta}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}O(1)+\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial r}\right|^{2}O(r^{2}).

Thus, the second, third and fourth estimates now follow from Lemma
14.4
. The first estimate is a restatement of the first estimate of Lemma
14.4
.
∎

Theorem 14.7
.

If
u:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
is the harmonic section of Theorem
9.8
, then we have the following estimates in the set
\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}
at a crossing (i.e. a subset of a set
\bar{\mathbb{D}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
of type (B)),

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{i}}{8\pi\rho^{2}}\right)dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}(-\log r^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{2}}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{j}}{8\pi r^{2}}\right)\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{\rho^{2}(-\log\rho^{2})^{2}}\wedge dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\rho}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}(-\log r^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial r}\right|^{2}\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{\rho^{2}(-\log\rho^{2})^{2}}\wedge dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\phi}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{j}}{2\pi}\right)\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{\rho^{2}}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}(-\log r^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{i}}{2\pi}\right)\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{\rho^{2}(-\log\rho^{2})^{2}}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

where
(z^{1}=\rho e^{i\phi},z^{2}=re^{i\theta})
are the holomorphic coordinates on
\bar{\mathbb{D}}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}
(cf. Definition
10.2
).

Proof.

The standard product coordinates of a set of type (B) are also the holomorphic coordinates.
Thus, these estimates follow immediately from
Lemma
13.9
and
(
11.32
).
∎

15.
Pluriharmonicity

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
9.9
. In other words, we show the pluriharmonicity of the harmonic section
u:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
of Theorem
9.8
in the case when the target space
\tilde{X}
is as in Theorem
1
.

For simplicity of exposition, we will assume that
\tilde{X}
is a complete, simply connected Kähler manifold of strong nonpositive curvature in the sense of Siu.

Thus, we can apply the variation of Siu’s Bochner formula, Theorem
4.9
.
More generally,
\tilde{X}
is a complete, simply connected Riemannian manifold of Hermitian negative curvature (cf. Remark
5.2
). Thus, the other cases follow analogously by using the variation of Sampson’s Bochner formula, Theorem
5.9
, and thus we omit the details.

The proof involves two steps: first is to show the integrability of all the terms in the Siu’s Bochner formula of Theorem
4.8
(cf. Subsection
15.1
) and second is to apply the modified Siu’s Bochner formula of Theorem
4.9
to prove each of these terms are actually equal to 0 (cf. Subsection
15.2
).
Our proof closely follows Mochizuki’s in
[
M
]
, but we apply the modified Bochner formula of Theorem
4.9
instead of the one used in his paper.

In Subsection
15.1
, we prove the integrability result. In the case when
M
is a closed Kähler manifold, Stoke’s theorem implies that the integration over the
\partial\bar{\partial}
-term of Siu’s formula (i.e. the left hand side of the formula from Theorem
4.8
) is equal to 0. On the other hand, we are considering non-compact domains. Thus, we must first multiply Siu’s formula by an appropriate cut-off function to apply Stoke’s Theorem. Unlike the compact case, this process yields terms that apriori seem to be an obstruction to pluriharmonicity. Nevertheless, this computation shows that the curvature function
Q
defined in Lemma
4.3
and the norm squared of the Hessian associated to
u
are integrable.

In Subsection
15.2
, we prove the pluriharmonicity result, Theorem
9.9
.
This is accomplished by combining the integrability results from Subsection
15.1
and the modified Bochner formula of Theorem
4.9
.

15.1.
Integrability of the curvature and Hessian terms from Siu’s formula

Let
u:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
be the harmonic section of Theorem
9.8
).
Denote by
\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}\rightarrow M
the vertical tangent bundle of
\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}\rightarrow M
. Let
\mathcal{V}^{\mathbb{C}}
denote the vertical complexified tangent bundle and

E=u^{*}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathbb{C}}).

The Levi-Civita connection on
\tilde{X}
induces a connection on
E
, and the
Bochner formulas of Chapter
2
can be interpreted in terms of sections of the appropriate bundles induced by
E
. In particular, the section
u
satisfies Siu’s Bochner formula (cf. Theorem
4.8
with
n=2
)

\displaystyle\partial\bar{\partial}\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(4\left|\partial_{E^{\prime}}\bar{\partial}u\right|^{2}+Q\right)\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}

where
Q
is the curvature term defined in Lemma
4.3
and
\omega
is the Kähler form of
M
. The goal is to show that the right hand side above is integrable in
M
. We first prove the following preliminary lemma.

Lemma 15.1
.

Let
V=\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}^{*}
be a set at a crossing (cf. Theorem
14.7
)
and
(z^{1}=\rho e^{i\phi},z^{2}=re^{i\theta})
be holomorphic coordinates in
V
.
If
F_{N}
is a sequence of functions defined on
V
satisfying the following:

(a)

\displaystyle{|F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})|\leq\frac{c}{(-\log r^{2})^{2}}}
for some constant
c>0

(b)

\displaystyle{c_{0}:=\int_{V}F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{\rho^{2}}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}}}
is independent of
N
.

(c)

For any
z^{2}\in\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{4}}^{*}
with
|z^{2}|=r
,

F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})=0
for
N
sufficiently large,

then

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{V}F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}}=\frac{c_{0}\mathsf{E}_{i}}{8\pi}.

Proof.

We first rewrite

(15.1)

\displaystyle\int_{V}F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{E}_{i}}{8\pi}\int_{V}F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{\rho^{2}}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle+\int_{V}F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{i}}{8\pi\rho^{2}}\right)dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}}.

The first term is equal to
\frac{c_{0}\mathsf{E}_{i}}{8\pi}
by assumption (b).
For the second term of (
15.10
), we first rewrite the integral as

\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{i}}{8\pi\rho^{2}}\right)\rho d\phi\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2ir^{2}}\right)d\rho.

By assumption (a), the integral inside the bracket, i.e. the function

(15.2)

r\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{i}}{8\pi\rho^{2}}\right)\rho d\phi\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2ir^{2}},

is bounded from above (independently of
N
) by a non-negative function

r\mapsto c\int_{\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}-\frac{\mathsf{E}_{i}}{8\pi\rho^{2}}\right)\rho d\phi\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{-2ir^{2}(-\log r^{2})^{2}}.

The above is non-negative by the definition of
\mathsf{E}_{i}
and integrable over the interval
[0,\frac{1}{4}]
by Lemma
14.7
.
Furthermore,
the function (
15.2
) converges to 0 for each
r\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
by assumption (c). Thus, Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem implies the result.
∎

Proposition 15.2
.

Let
u:M\rightarrow\tilde{M}\times_{\rho}\tilde{X}
be the harmonic section of Theorem
9.8
.
If

\tilde{X}
is a Kähler manifold with strong nonpositive curvature in the sense of Siu, then

\int_{M}Q\,\omega^{2}<\infty\mbox{ and }\int_{M}|\partial_{E^{\prime}}\bar{\partial}u|^{2}=\int_{M}|\partial_{E^{\prime\prime}}\bar{\partial}\bar{u}|^{2}\,\omega^{2}<\infty.

Proof.

Let
\eta:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,1]
be a non-increasing, non-negative smooth function
satisfying

\eta(x)=1\mbox{ for }0\leq x\leq\frac{1}{2},\ \ \eta(x)=0\mbox{ for }\frac{2}{3}\leq x<\infty.

For
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
, define a cut-off function

(15.3)

\chi_{N}:M\rightarrow[0,1],\ \ \ \chi_{N}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle{\prod_{j=1}^{L}\eta\left(N^{-1}\log|\sigma_{j}|_{h_{j}}^{-2}\right)}&\mbox{in }\displaystyle{\bigcup_{j=1}^{L}{\mathcal{D}}_{j}^{*}}\\
1&\mbox{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

where
\sigma_{j}
is the canonical section and
h_{j}
is the Hermitian metric on
{\mathcal{O}}(\Sigma_{j})
(cf. Section
10
). Note that we use (
10.8
) to identify the neighborhood
{\mathcal{D}}_{j}
of the zero section of
{\mathcal{O}}(\Sigma_{j})
with the neighborhood of
\Sigma_{j}
in
\bar{M}
.
Multiplying the Siu-Bochner formula of Theorem
4.8
by
\chi_{N}
, integrating over
M
and applying integration by parts, we obtain

(15.4)

\int_{M}\partial\bar{\partial}\chi_{N}\wedge\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{M}\chi_{N}\left(4\left|\partial_{E^{\prime}}\bar{\partial}u\right|^{2}+Q\right)\omega^{2}.

By the assumption that
\tilde{X}
has strong non-positive curvature in the sense of Siu (cf. Lemma
4.3
), the right hand side of the above equality is non-negative.

Let
V
be either a set
\Omega\times\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}
away from the crossings (cf. Theorem
14.6
) or a set

\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}^{*}
at a crossing (cf. Theorem
14.7
).
Since
\partial\bar{\partial}\chi_{N}
is supported in the finite union of such sets for sufficiently large
N
, it suffices to prove

(15.5)

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{V}\partial\bar{\partial}\chi_{N}\wedge\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}<\infty

for either
V=\Omega\times\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}
or
V=\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}^{*}
.
Throughout this proof of (
15.5
), we will use
c
to denote a generic positive constant that may change from line to line but is independent of
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

First, consider the subset

V=\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}^{*}
near a crossing with local holomorphic coordinates
(z^{1}=\rho e^{i\phi},z^{2}=re^{i\theta})
. In
V
and for
N
sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\chi_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta\left(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2}\right)\eta\left(-N^{-1}\log r^{2}\right).

The support of
\eta^{\prime}\left(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2}\right)
and
\eta^{\prime\prime}\left(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2}\right)
is contained in

(15.6)

W_{N}:=\left\{\frac{1}{2}\leq-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2}\leq\frac{2}{3}\right\}

and the support of
\eta^{\prime}\left(-N^{-1}\log r^{2}\right)
and
\eta^{\prime\prime}\left(-N^{-1}\log r^{2}\right)
is contained in

(15.7)

V_{N}:=\left\{\frac{1}{2}\leq-N^{-1}\log r^{2}\leq\frac{2}{3}\right\}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle(-\log\rho^{2})\left|\frac{\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})}{N}\right|\leq c,

\displaystyle(-\log r^{2})\left|\frac{\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})}{N}\right|\leq c

(15.8)

\displaystyle(-\log\rho^{2})^{2}\left|\frac{\eta^{\prime\prime}(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})}{N^{2}}\right|\leq c,

\displaystyle(-\log r^{2})^{2}\left|\frac{\eta^{\prime\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})}{N^{2}}\right|\leq c.

We have

(15.9)

\displaystyle\partial\bar{\partial}\chi_{N}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})\eta^{\prime\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{N^{2}r^{2}}

\displaystyle\ +\eta^{\prime\prime}(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})\eta(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{N^{2}\rho^{2}}

\displaystyle\ \ +\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{N^{2}z^{1}\bar{z}^{2}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{N^{2}\bar{z}^{1}z^{2}}.

Using (
15.9
), we write the integral of (
15.5
) as the sum
(i)+(ii)+(iii)+(iv)

where

\displaystyle(i)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{V}\eta(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})\eta^{\prime\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{N^{2}r^{2}}\wedge\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}

\displaystyle(ii)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{V}\eta^{\prime\prime}(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})\eta(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{N^{2}\rho^{2}}\wedge\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}

\displaystyle(iii)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{V}\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{N^{2}z^{1}\bar{z}^{2}}\wedge\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}

\displaystyle(iv)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{V}\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{N^{2}\bar{z}^{1}z^{2}}\wedge\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}.

First, consider the integral
(i)
. Using the identity

\langle\frac{\partial u}{\partial{\bar{z}^{\alpha}}},\frac{\partial u}{{\partial{\bar{z}^{\beta}}}}\rangle d\bar{z}^{\alpha}\wedge dz^{\beta}=h_{i\bar{j}}\frac{\partial u^{i}}{\partial{\bar{z}^{\alpha}}}\overline{\frac{\partial u^{j}}{{\partial{\bar{z}^{\beta}}}}}d\bar{z}^{\alpha}\wedge dz^{\beta}=\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\},

we have

(15.10)

(i)=\int_{V}\eta(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})\frac{\eta^{\prime\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})}{N^{2}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}}.

We now check that

F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})=\eta(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})\frac{\eta^{\prime\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})}{N^{2}}

satisfies the assumptions (a), (b) and (c) of Lemma
15.1
.
First,
F(z^{1},z^{2})
satisfies assumption (a) of Lemma
15.1
by (
15.8
).
Next, we will check that the function
F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})
also satisfies assumption (b) of Lemma
15.1
.
Indeed, after a change of variables,

(15.11)

t=-N^{-1}\log\rho\ \mbox{ and }\ s=-N^{-1}\log r,

we obtain

\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{N\rho^{2}}=-2i\frac{d\rho\wedge d\phi}{N\rho}=2idt\wedge d\phi\ \mbox{ and }\ \frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{Nr^{2}}=-2i\frac{dr\wedge d\theta}{Nr}=2ids\wedge d\theta.

Thus,

\displaystyle c_{0}:=\int_{V}\eta(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})\frac{\eta^{\prime\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})}{N^{2}}\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{\rho^{2}}\wedge\frac{d\bar{z}^{2}\wedge dz^{2}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}\eta(2t)dt\int_{\frac{1}{4}}^{\frac{1}{3}}\eta^{\prime\prime}(2s)ds=c\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}\eta(2t)dt\cdot\left(\eta^{\prime}(\frac{2}{3})-\eta^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2})\right)=0.

Finally,
F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})
satisfies assumption (c) of Lemma
15.1
by (
15.7
).
By applying Lemma
15.1
, we conclude

(15.12)

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}|(i)|=0.

The same argument also implies

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}|(ii)|=0.

We will now bound the term
(iii)
. Indeed, we can rewrite

(15.13)

\displaystyle|(iii)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{V}\frac{\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})}{N}\frac{\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})}{N}\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{z^{1}\bar{z}^{2}}\wedge\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{V}\left|\langle\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}},\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{2}}\rangle\right|\frac{\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})}{N}\frac{\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})}{N}\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{\rho}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{V_{N}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|^{2}\left(\frac{\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log\rho^{2})}{N}\right)^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle\ \ +\int_{W_{N}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{2}}\right|^{2}\left(\frac{\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})}{N}\right)^{2}\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{\rho^{2}}\wedge dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}.

For the first integral on the right hand side of (
15.13
), we let

F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})=\chi_{V_{N}}\left(\frac{\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})}{N}\right)^{2}

where
\chi_{V_{N}}
is the characteristic function of
V_{N}
.
First,
F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})
satisfies assumption (a) of Lemma
15.1
by (
15.8
),.
Next, we check that it satisfies assumption (b) of Lemma
15.1
. Indeed, using the substitution (
15.11
),

\displaystyle c_{0}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int_{V}\chi_{V_{N}}\left(\frac{\eta^{\prime}(-N^{-1}\log r^{2})}{N}\right)^{2}\frac{dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}}{\rho^{2}}\wedge\frac{dz^{2}\wedge d\bar{z}^{2}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\int_{\frac{1}{4}}^{\frac{1}{3}}dt\ \int_{\frac{1}{4}}^{\frac{1}{3}}(\eta^{\prime}(2s))^{2}ds.

Finally,
F_{N}(z^{1},z^{2})
satisfies assumption (c) of Lemma
15.1
by (
15.7
).
Thus, the second integral on the right hand side of (
15.13
) limits to
\frac{c_{0}\mathsf{E}_{i}}{8\pi}
as
N\rightarrow\infty
by Lemma
15.1
. Analogously, the second integral on the right hand side of (
15.13
) limits to
\frac{c_{0}\mathsf{E}_{j}}{8\pi}
as
N\rightarrow\infty
.
Thus, we have shown

(15.14)

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}|(iii)|=\frac{c_{0}(\mathsf{E}_{i}+\mathsf{E}_{j})}{8\pi}.

Same argument shows

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}|(iv)|=\frac{c_{0}(\mathsf{E}_{i}+\mathsf{E}_{j})}{8\pi}.

Summing the limits of
(i)
,
(ii)
,
(iii)
and
(iv)
, we conclude that (
15.5
) is satisfied in the case
V=\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}^{*}\times\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{\frac{1}{4}}^{*}
.

Next, consider set
V=\Omega\times\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\frac{1}{4}}
away from the crossings with holomorphic coordinates
(z^{1},\zeta=re^{i\theta})
.
In
V
and for sufficiently large
N
,

(15.15)

\chi_{N}(z^{1},\zeta)=\eta\left(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2}\right).

We compute

(15.16)

\displaystyle\partial\bar{\partial}\chi_{N}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta^{\prime\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N^{2}}\left(\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{|\zeta|^{2}}+\frac{\partial b\wedge\bar{\partial}b}{b^{2}}-\frac{\partial b\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{b\bar{\zeta}}-\frac{d\zeta\wedge\bar{\partial}b}{\zeta b}\right)

\displaystyle\ +\frac{\eta^{\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N}\partial\bar{\partial}\log b.

The support of
\eta^{\prime}
and
\eta^{\prime\prime}
is contained in

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{2}\leq N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2}\leq\frac{2}{3}\right\},

which is contained in the set

(15.17)

V_{N}=\Omega\times\mathbb{D}_{z^{2},c_{1}e^{-\frac{N}{3}},c_{2}e^{-\frac{N}{4}}}

for appropriate constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
depending only on
b
.
Therefore,

(15.18)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\eta^{\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{\log br^{-2}}\leq\frac{c}{-\log r^{2}},

(15.19)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\eta^{\prime\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{(\log br^{-2})^{2}}\leq\frac{c}{(-\log r^{2})^{2}}.

Using (
15.16
), we write the integral of (
15.5
) as the sum
(I)+(II)+(III)+(IV)+(V)
. For the integral
(I)
, we write

\displaystyle|(I)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{V}\frac{\eta^{\prime\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N^{2}}\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{|\zeta|^{2}}\wedge\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\int_{V_{N}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{r^{2}(-\log r^{2})^{2}}\ \ \ (\mbox{by (\ref{cleverbd2}))}.

By Theorem
14.6
,

\int_{V}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{r^{2}(-\log r^{2})^{2}}<\infty.

Thus, Lebesgue’s dominated convergence Theorem implies

(15.20)

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(I)=0.

For the integral
(II)
, we write

\displaystyle|(II)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{V}\frac{\eta^{\prime\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N^{2}}\frac{\partial b\wedge\bar{\partial}b}{b^{2}}\wedge\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\int_{V_{N}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{(-\log r^{2})^{2}}+\int_{V_{N}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{(-\log r^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ (\mbox{by}\ \frac{\partial b}{b}=O(1)\ \mbox{and }(\ref{cleverbd2})).

By Theorem
14.6
, we can apply an analogous argument to (
15.20
) to conclude

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(II)=0.

In order to estimate
(III)
, notice that

\displaystyle d\bar{\zeta}\wedge\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}=d\bar{\zeta}\wedge\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|^{2}d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge d{z}^{1}+\langle\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}},\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\rangle d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge d\zeta\right).

Thus,

\displaystyle|(III)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{V}\frac{\eta^{\prime\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N^{2}}\frac{\partial b\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{b\bar{\zeta}}\wedge\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\int_{V_{N}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|^{2}+\left|\langle\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}},\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\rangle\right|\right)dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{r(-\log r^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ ({\mbox{by}\ \frac{\partial b}{b}=O(1)\mbox{ and }(\ref{cleverbd2}))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\int_{V_{N}}r\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|^{2}+\left|\langle\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}},\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\rangle\right|\right)\ dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{r^{2}(-\log r^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\int_{V_{N}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|^{2}+r^{2}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\right|^{2}\right)dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{r^{2}(-\log r^{2})^{2}}\ \ \mbox{(by Cauchy-Schwartz)}.

By Theorem
14.6
, we can apply an analogous argument to (
15.20
) to conclude

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(III)=0.

Similarly,

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(IV)=0.

We thus conclude

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}|(I)|+|(II)|+|(III)|+|(IV)|=0.

Next,

\displaystyle(V)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{V}\frac{\eta^{\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N}\partial\bar{\partial}\log b\wedge\{\bar{\partial}u,\bar{\partial}u\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{V}\frac{\eta^{\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N}\frac{\partial^{2}\log b}{\partial z^{1}\partial\bar{z}^{1}}dz^{1}\wedge d{\bar{z}}^{1}\wedge\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\right|^{2}d\bar{\zeta}\wedge d{\zeta}

\displaystyle\ +\int_{V}\frac{\eta^{\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N}\frac{\partial^{2}\log b}{\partial\zeta\partial\bar{\zeta}}d\zeta\wedge d{\bar{\zeta}}\wedge\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|^{2}d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge dz^{1}

\displaystyle\ \ +\int_{V}\frac{\eta^{\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N}\frac{\partial^{2}\log b}{\partial z^{1}\partial\bar{\zeta}}dz^{1}\wedge d{\bar{\zeta}}\wedge<\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}},\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}>d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge d\zeta

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\int_{V}\frac{\eta^{\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N}\frac{\partial^{2}\log b}{\partial\zeta\partial\bar{z}^{1}}d\zeta\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge<\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{\zeta}},\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}>d\bar{\zeta}\wedge d{z}^{1}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle(V)_{1}+(V)_{2}+(V)_{3}+(V)_{4}.

We estimate

\displaystyle|(V)_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\int_{V}\left|\frac{\eta^{\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N}\right|\left|\frac{\partial^{2}\log b}{\partial z^{1}\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\int_{V_{N}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|^{2}dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{(-\log r^{2})}

\displaystyle\left(\mbox{by}\ \frac{\partial^{2}\log b}{\partial\zeta\partial\bar{\zeta}}=O(1)\ \mbox{ and }(\ref{cleverbd1})\right)

\displaystyle|(V)_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{V}\left|\frac{\eta^{\prime}(N^{-1}\log b|\zeta|^{-2})}{N}\right|\left|\frac{\partial^{2}\log b}{\partial z^{1}\partial\bar{\zeta}}\right|\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\right|dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge d{\bar{\zeta}}\wedge d\zeta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\int_{V_{N}}\frac{1}{(-\log r^{2})}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\right|dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge d{\bar{\zeta}}\wedge d\zeta

\displaystyle\left(\mbox{by}\ \frac{\partial^{2}\log b}{\partial\zeta\partial\bar{\zeta}}=O(1)\ \mbox{and }\ (\ref{cleverbd1})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\int_{V_{N}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}}\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{(-\log r^{2})^{2}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\right|^{2}\right)dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}

and similarly

\displaystyle|(V)_{4}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\int_{V_{N}}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial z^{1}}\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{(-\log r^{2})^{2}}\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial\zeta}\right|^{2}\right)dz^{1}\wedge d\bar{z}^{1}\wedge d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}.

With these estimates, we can argue as in the proof of (
15.20
) to conclude

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(V)_{2}+(V)_{3}+(V)_{4}=0.

We are left to compute
